=== argparse 2.0.1 (Python-2.0) ===
A. HISTORY OF THE SOFTWARE
==========================

Python was created in the early 1990s by Guido van Rossum at Stichting
Mathematisch Centrum (CWI, see http://www.cwi.nl) in the Netherlands
as a successor of a language called ABC.  Guido remains Python's
principal author, although it includes many contributions from others.

In 1995, Guido continued his work on Python at the Corporation for
National Research Initiatives (CNRI, see http://www.cnri.reston.va.us)
in Reston, Virginia where he released several versions of the
software.

In May 2000, Guido and the Python core development team moved to
BeOpen.com to form the BeOpen PythonLabs team.  In October of the same
year, the PythonLabs team moved to Digital Creations, which became
Zope Corporation.  In 2001, the Python Software Foundation (PSF, see
https://www.python.org/psf/) was formed, a non-profit organization
created specifically to own Python-related Intellectual Property.
Zope Corporation was a sponsoring member of the PSF.

All Python releases are Open Source (see http://www.opensource.org for
the Open Source Definition).  Historically, most, but not all, Python
releases have also been GPL-compatible; the table below summarizes
the various releases.

    Release         Derived     Year        Owner       GPL-
                    from                                compatible? (1)

    0.9.0 thru 1.2              1991-1995   CWI         yes
    1.3 thru 1.5.2  1.2         1995-1999   CNRI        yes
    1.6             1.5.2       2000        CNRI        no
    2.0             1.6         2000        BeOpen.com  no
    1.6.1           1.6         2001        CNRI        yes (2)
    2.1             2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         no
    2.0.1           2.0+1.6.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.1           2.1+2.0.1   2001        PSF         yes
    2.1.2           2.1.1       2002        PSF         yes
    2.1.3           2.1.2       2002        PSF         yes
    2.2 and above   2.1.1       2001-now    PSF         yes

Footnotes:

(1) GPL-compatible doesn't mean that we're distributing Python under
    the GPL.  All Python licenses, unlike the GPL, let you distribute
    a modified version without making your changes open source.  The
    GPL-compatible licenses make it possible to combine Python with
    other software that is released under the GPL; the others don't.

(2) According to Richard Stallman, 1.6.1 is not GPL-compatible,
    because its license has a choice of law clause.  According to
    CNRI, however, Stallman's lawyer has told CNRI's lawyer that 1.6.1
    is "not incompatible" with the GPL.

Thanks to the many outside volunteers who have worked under Guido's
direction to make these releases possible.

   TERMS AND CONDITIONS FOR USE, REPRODUCTION, AND DISTRIBUTION

   1. Definitions.

      "License" shall mean the terms and conditions for use, reproduction,

      "Licensor" shall mean the copyright owner or entity authorized by

TOUCHLINE — FOOTBALL REFERENCE DATA

2026/27 snapshot retrieved 2026-11-08. No live transfer connection. Existing saved careers retain their own reference data.

English factual roster fields: vaastav/Fantasy-Premier-League, data/2026-27/players_raw.csv and teams.csv, repository MIT. Only selectable football-player records retained; position groups adapted to Touchline. No FPL photographs or logos used.
https://github.com/vaastav/Fantasy-Premier-League

Fixtures and club identities: openfootball/football.json, 2026-27, CC0-1.0. Real scores were discarded. Calendar is an initial-season reference, not a live fixture feed.
https://github.com/openfootball/football.json

Portuguese squad/birth-date factual extraction: Wikipedia contributors, CC BY-SA 4.0. Revision URLs below identify the consulted pages and their contributor history. Structured reference extraction adapted to Touchline position groups; unknown ages remain null. This reference dataset is available under the stated source terms; game source code is separate. No official scouting ratings or market values are copied.
https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/

Data snapshots, SHA256 and transformations are recorded in server/data/current-provenance.json and scripts/build-current-data.py in the editable source. Ratings, potential, price and results are Touchline simulation parameters, not official statistics. Some photographs date from other seasons/kits; their source-declared year and individual license are listed separately. No endorsement or partnership is implied.

ARCHIVED 2024/25 DATA:
TOUCHLINE — HISTORICAL FOOTBALL DATA

Fixtures and club names: openfootball/football.json, season 2024-25. CC0-1.0.
https://github.com/openfootball/football.json

English player names, historical club association, birth years and position categories: vaastav/Fantasy-Premier-League, data/2024-25/players_raw.csv and teams.csv. Repository MIT license reproduced below. Only factual fields used; no photographs or official logos are reproduced.
https://github.com/vaastav/Fantasy-Premier-League

Portuguese playable squads: manual historical curation for Benfica, Porto, Sporting and Braga, selection from the 2024/25 season rather than a current roster or a single transfer-date snapshot. Birth-year ages approximate. Other Portuguese clubs have real identities and simulated strengths, without claimed complete player squads.

Ratings, potential, price, opponent strengths and all simulated results are Touchline game parameters, not official football or scouting ratings. No affiliation with leagues, clubs, players or FPL is implied.

Photographs: individually admitted images are listed in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES and in-game Preferences. Original vector portraits are illustrations and are labelled as such in historical careers.

TOUCHLINE · ORIGINAL GENERATED PORTRAIT ART

Created 7 November 2026 with OpenAI ImageGen for the original Touchline demo.
Two 4x4 atlases: 16 original fictional character portraits; 16 original editorial illustrations of public footballers. These are generated artwork, not photographs, official images, or documentary likenesses. No affiliation or endorsement is implied. In-game labels distinguish generated/illustrative portraits from individually verified photographs.

Editorial illustration atlas, row-major order:
Mohamed Salah; Erling Haaland; Bruno Fernandes; Bukayo Saka;
Ángel Di María; Nicolás Otamendi; Diogo Costa; Viktor Gyökeres;
Morten Hjulmand; Francisco Trincão; Orkun Kökçü; Vangelis Pavlidis;
Virgil van Dijk; Cole Palmer; Son Heung-min; Luis Díaz.

Normalized to 1024x1024 WebP; source identifiers and SHA256 are recorded in src/assets/portraits/provenance.json in the editable project. Generic SVG portraits remain for historical players without an admitted photograph or curated illustration. No official club logos or assets from a commercial football game are included.

App icons (180/192/512 PNG): raster exports of the original Touchline vector mark, generated locally with scripts/create-app-icons.mjs. No external artwork.

Player photographs v0.16.1: 16 individually credited CC BY / CC BY-SA photographs. Per-file authors, sources, license URLs, modifications and SHA256 are listed in THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES and in-game Preferences. No endorsement is implied. The photo may show a different season or kit from the historical game archive.

Player photographs v0.17: 24 athletes with admitted photographs. Eight additions and two superseding files; source-declared photo year is recorded and shown. Images are not all from 2026; no current-kit identity retouching was performed. Current per-file license notices take precedence over the historical collection counts above.
